Synopsis of Observations

1.1 Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts Expenditure Exceeded the Budgeted Amount Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
More specific (micro level) audit findings, along-with recommendations thereon, are as following: Repeated Observations (Un-Resolved) Budgetary limits should be kept in view while making spending. Budgeting and analysis of variances therefrom, are useful monitoring and control tools that can help relevant authorities in the process of effective management and good governance. The review of system generated “Budget Variance Sheets” revealed that overspending was made under certain head of accounts. Few of such instances are shared as Annexure-A.
1.2 Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts Budget for Student Expenditure Remained Unutilized Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
As per Company policy, savings under the category of student expenses should be avoided. During review of “Budget Variance Sheets”, it was observed that budget for student expenditure remained unutilized in certain heads. Few of such instances are shared as Annexure-B.
1.3 Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts Withholding Tax Deposited with Delay Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
As per Company policy, tax withheld from suppliers should be deposited within seven days. During review of ledger, delays were observed. Few of the instances are shared as Annexure-C.
1.4 Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts Reimbursements not Timely Processed by RO Low-Med 🔁 Repeated RO
Observation:
Branches are allocated a limited budget for each head of account, and reimbursement claims should be processed in a timely manner. However, based on the detail provided by the branch, delays were observed in the processing of claims. Few of the instances have been shared as Annexure-D.
1.5 Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts Balance Confirmation Certificates not Provided Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
For balance confirmation purposes, the Branch was requested for the balance confirmation certificates from the vendors with whom the business activities were done and staff to whom advances were issued (during the audit period). However, the balance confirmation certificates were awaited till audit completion.
1.6 Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts Accounting Procedure not Followed Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
All the supporting documents must be presented to the Branch Head along with the cheque, to ensure the compliance of procedures. However, it was observed that the Cheques were apparently prepared and issued without printing the related bank payment vouchers. Seemingly the vouchers were printed after making the transaction. Few of the instances may be referred below: [Sr. No. | Ref ID | Voucher Date | Amount(Rs.) | PrintingDate | Delay indays

1.7 Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts Vendors Registration not Performed Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
As per policy, procurement shall be made from registered vendors. The registered vendor is a vendor for which Vendor Master Data is available in Vendor Register for Branches, based on approved VRF. However, the practice of vendor registration was not being properly followed, and the vendor register was not maintained.
1.8 Recruitment, Payroll & Employee Record Employees Record of Social Security not Available Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
As per Punjab Govt directions, employees having salary from Rs. 41,000 (or as prescribed by the notification issued time to time) should be registered with Punjab Employees Social Security Institution (providing hospitalization and dispensary facilities etc.) It was observed that PESSI record for the payments etc., was not available.

Observation:
Attendance should be properly marked by all employees. As per BEAMS record, it was noted that outgoing attendance was not marked in certain cases. Due to the issue, compliance of office timings cannot be verified. The matter may affect also their Leave Encashment. Few of the instances has been shared in Annexure-F.

1.23 Fixed Assets Inadequate Care of Fixed Assets Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
The branch was found to be exercising insufficient care in managing and maintaining its fixed assets. Lapses were noted in proper handling, preventive maintenance, and timely reporting of damages or repair requirements. Such deficiencies may lead to premature deterioration, higher repair costs, and a reduced useful life of the assets. For reference please refer to attached pictures:
1.24 Fixed Assets Physical Verification Report not Available Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
Company record should be maintained with due care. It was observed that the physical verification reports were not available with the Branch. Due to the unavailability of record compliance of the process could not be verified.
1.25 Fixed Assets Memorandum Registers not Properly Maintained Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
As per policy memorandum stock registers should be maintained by branch. However, such registers e.g. Fixed Assets having cost less than the threshold value (PKR 20,000), Teaching Aid Register (Early Years furniture etc.), and the Music Register etc. were not properly maintained. The registers were not properly updated.
1.26 Fixed Assets Inventory Record of Landlord’s Assets not Provided Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
To avoid any confusion at the time of vacation in the building, a list of fixed assets belonging to the building owner should be available in the branch. It was observed during the audit that Branch had no record of the Landlord’s assets lying in the premises of the branch.
1.27 Library High Item Per Child Book Ratio in Library Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
As per standard practice of the company, item-per-child should be 15. However, during the audit, it was informed that the item-per-child in the Library was 29.
1.28 Library Overdue Books Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
Books should be returned within stipulated time. During the audit certain books were appearing as overdue. For few of such instances (ranging 34 to 2,531 days) please refer Annexure-H.
1.29 Others Demolition Register not Prepared Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
As per policy, a demolition register should be maintained to record scrap generated from demolition, repair activities, and related works. However, as informed by concerned person, the branch was not maintaining the demolition register, as specified in the policy.
1.30 Others Document Tracking Sheet not in Practice Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
For better control over movement of documents in different offices, “Document Tracking Sheet” should be attached with the important documents. However, the branch was not in practice of attaching the “Document Tracking Sheet” with the documents being forwarded to different departments.
1.31 Others Validated Total Paintable Area Measurement not Available Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Observation:
As paint work is a regular annual exercise in the branch, record of total paintable area duly endorsed by Works Department and OFA (Officer Facility Administration) should be available in branch to avoid any ambiguity at the time of awarding paint work contracts and its subsequent verification of work done. It was observed that the available document was annual paint contractor’s measurement sheet which was not even verified and endorsed by Works Department and OFA (Officer Facility Administration) as “Total Paintable Area of the Branch”.
2.1 Branch Imprest, Payments& Accounts Significant Anomalies in Processing of Paint Bills Med-High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
During the audit, it was observed that paint bills were processed without proper verification of measurements claimed by paint contractor and related deductions. Few of such discrepancies included: Glass affixed in doors was not deducted from the painted area. White boards installed on the walls were also not properly deducted from the painted area. For example, in the branch some of the whiteboards were affixed partially on surfaces painted with emulsion and partially on surfaces painted with enamel. However, the deductions were not made in accordance with the applicable paint type (i.e. emulsion or enamel)/rate. As informed by Branch Personnel: Verification of the paint material by minimum two members of the Purchase Committee (PC) was not conducted to ensure that the supplied material complied with the approved specifications. The paint brand and quality were also not verified by AMCO, prior to application. The paint bills were processed without verification by AMCO. Signed delivery challans were not available to verify documentation regarding the receipt of materials. No documentary evidence was available to verify that approval had been obtained for the application of the second coat after completion of the first, as required under the prescribed process. Verification of the application of double coats was not carried out by the Admin and Accounts. Weather Shield was applied in certain areas before the prescribed period (of three years); however, no exception approval from the competent authority was obtained for the early reapplication. Paint Brand was not mentioned on the bills, and quotations.
2.2 Branch Imprest, Payments& Accounts Excess Payment (Photocopy Bills) Med-High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Bills should be processed after proper checking. Photocopy bills (for the period August 2025 to January 2026) were reviewed in coordination with the Branch Accountant. It was observed that in the photocopy bills (for October 2025 and November 2025), the vendor charged excess copies. Specifically 1,710 excess copies were charged in November 2025 and 100 additional copies were charged in October 2025 (at the rate of Rs. 3.90 per copy) leading to an excess payment of Rs. 7,059.
2.3 Branch Imprest, Payments& Accounts Utility Connection Share not Properly Collected Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
As per standard company practice, utility charges should be regularly collected from relevant parties. However, according to the detail provided by Branch, such collection was not being done properly on a regular basis.
2.4 Branch Imprest, Payments& Accounts Reference of Folio Number not Available on Relevant Bills Med-High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
To maintain effective control, branches are required to keep stock registers (For the purpose, standardized "Stock Registers" should be used including columns for receipt, issuance, balance, and remarks etc). Additionally, when submitting bills for such items the reference of folio number should be mentioned to ensure that stock register is updated. However, it was observed that such process was not followed. Furthermore, as upon checking, it was found that the sports items were also not recorded in the stock register. Few of such bills may be referred below: [Vendor | Amount (Rs.) | Date

2.6 Branch Imprest, Payments& Accounts Periodic Evaluation of Vendors not Performed Med-High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Evaluation of vendors should be conducted as prescribed in the policy. However, it was observed that this practice was not being followed.
2.7 Branch Imprest, Payments& Accounts Specimen Signature (of Branch Personnel) not Available Low 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Specimen of signatures help in identifying signatures on bills, purchase requisition, purchase order etc. A copy of the signature specimen should also be given to RO because they check Imprests, Adjustments, and Reimbursements etc. During the audit, it was noted that no such specimen signatures were available with branch. For example (but not limited to) Head of School, Section Head(s), Branch Purchase Committee Members, Accountant, or any other personnel signing the bills and related documents.
2.8 Branch Imprest, Payments& Accounts Incongruities in Preparation & Filing of Purchase Orders Low 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Purchase orders are basic document for purchases and acquisition of services. As per the standard practice, the PO/WO should be numbered, and a copy should be filed in sequential order. In addition to this, the company policy prescribes to obtain vendor acceptance, for written agreement of vendor(s) to the prescribed terms and conditions. However, it was identified that such practices were not being properly followed.
2.9 Cash & Bank Discrepancies in Cash Collection Activities High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
During the audit, significant discrepancies and non-compliance with prescribed policies were observed in cash collection and related activity management. Such as: No cash collection record was available prior to September 2023. A fake quotation was found attached with the transportation bill of Ms. Ali Tours (November 2025). Expenses were not submitted to the Regional Office within the specified intervals (e.g., three field trips of November 2025). As per BEAMS Rs. 113,550 was balance from different activities which was not adjusted since long. 2.10 Cash & Bank Significant Anomalies in Bake Sales Record High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
During the audit, significant non-compliance with prescribed policies and deficiencies in documentation related to Bake Sale activities were identified. These included: Detail of Bake Sales was not properly maintained. Bake Sale Activity for 2022 was still open in “Cash Collection Module”, and there was no record available. For the year 2025 & 2026: Committee formation details for Bake Sale activities were not available. Serial Number Receipt Book record was not available. Detailed cash collection record was not available for verification, including (but not limited to): Collections, donations, and the balances etc. Compliance with price ceiling of PKR 200 per item. Compliance with student cash limit of PKR 500 per student. Activities were not recorded in “Cash Collection Module”. Approval from BH (after closure of activity) was not available. Receipt for Rs. 50,000 donated to SKMT (Year 2025) was not available. Balances from Bake Sale e.g. Rs. 2,420 (Feb 2025), and Rs. 20,710 (Jan 2026) were not recorded in module.

2.26 Student Record Fee Concession Approvals not Properly Filed Low 🆕 New BH
Observation:
As per standard practice, fee concession documents / approvals should be filed in student files so that these can be referred at the time of need. While scrutinizing the student files, and as shared by relevant person, it was observed that the approvals of fee concessions were not properly attached in files. If such an approval is obtained online, extract / printout / reference of online approval may be attached in the file. RecommendationAs per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/admission-policy/students-registration-and-admissions/5/- 6. Student Files: “A detailed Student File Check List. This checklist shall be pasted on the inside of the file cover. The PRO shall ensure that all the required documents are filed in personal files of students as per the check list pasted in the file.” As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/wp-content/uploads/2022/08/SOM003-Student-Files-Check-List.pdf: “15: Fee Concession Order issued by ……”

Efficient utilization of ISM/BEAMS should be ensured. Proper access rights and training should also be provided in this regard. Additionally, the IT department may be contacted to request the generation of specific additional reports as needed. It was observed that for branch PROs, no intimation, report, or pop-up is generated (branch-wise) through ISM/BEAMS, for the preparation and dispatch of students' security refund cheques. 2.31 Fixed Assets Aspects of Assets not Mentioned in Fixed Asset Register Low-Med 🆕 New RO
Observation:
Aspect of assets (Brand, Make, Model etc.) should be mentioned in fixed asset register. It was observed during physical verification and scrutiny of fixed asset record, that only category of assets was mentioned, aspects containing description of the assets were not available in fixed asset register of the branch.
2.32 Fixed Assets Discrepancies in FA Register Columns Low-Med 🆕 New RO
Observation:
As per company practice, complete information should be mentioned in fixed asset register. However, it was observed that in certain instances few columns were left blank e.g. GRN number, Supplier name, Invoice number, and PO reference etc.
2.33 Library Delay in Return of Books by Students Med-High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Books should be returned within stipulated time. It was observed during the audit that books were not returned within stipulated time by students. Few of such instances are mentioned as Annexure-J.
2.34 Library Delay in Return of Books by Staff Med-High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Books should be returned within stipulated time. It was observed during the audit that books were not returned within stipulated time by staff. Few of such instances are mentioned as Annexure-K.
2.35 Library Dispose Books Still Appearing in Library Module Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
The Library Module should be updated on timely basis. Disposal of books should be processed in module immediately upon approval of AIDN by RO. During audit we found that many books were appearing in library module but physically not available in the branch. On further inquiry, it was observed that, these books have been disposed-off but their disposal was not yet processed in Library Module. Few of such instances are mentioned as Annexure-L.
2.36 Library Torn Out Books Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
As per company policy, Librarian should check the books on receipt from students / teachers and get mended / repaired if needed. It was observed that, certain library books were with torn out pages which needed either repair or should have been discarded. Few of such is as under: [Sr. No. | Accession# | Book title

2.40 Library Minutes of Librarian Meeting File not Available Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Minutes of the librarian meeting with RO Librarian should befiled by branch librarian. During the audit it was observed that minutes of librarian meeting was not filed. RecommendationRecord should be properly maintained. As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/library-manuals-2/15-record-keeping/ - 15. Record Keeping: “Minutes of the Library Coordination Meeting: The Regional Librarian should call a meeting of school librarians once or twice in an academic year. All librarians are given minutes of that meeting. The librarian should file these.”

Observation:

2.42 Library File of Reminders not Maintained Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
File for reminder of outstanding books should be maintained by the branch. During audit it was observed that such was not available.
2.43 Library Discrepancies with Library Module (KOHA) Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Efficient utilization of library software (KOHA) should be ensured. Proper trainings and proper rights of the library module (KOHA) should also be provided to the librarians to ensure this. Certain discrepancies were observed and subsequently confirmed by the branch librarian in discussion which seemed to be creating difficulties for the librarians in performing their day to day operations regarding library module. Such as: For branch librarian rights to view & access books were not restricted to specific branch. Auto generation of Over-due books notification (email) was not in practice. Auto generation of Issued & Returned books notification (email) was not in practice. Stocktaking through bar codes and subsequently stocktaking report generation.
2.44 Safety & Security Discrepancies in School Security Med-High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Branch security is an important area and should be addressed accordingly. Certain areas were identified which were discussed with the Security Supervisor. Concerned security department may consider the point(s) during planning for safety and security of the premises, with support of RO Security Department. Few of the same are stated below: Visitor entries were not properly recorded. In certain instances, CNIC Number, Phone Number, Visitor Signature, Purpose of visit, etc. we’re missing. Barbed Wire: Was rusted. Gaps on certain places. Common boundary walls of attached neighbouring buildings, were soft areas for security. Concerned security department may consider the point during planning for security and safety of the premises. There were miscellaneous construction, fixtures & trees along parameter wall, which could provide assistance for negotiation. Walk Through Gate and checking staff in the close proximity, therefor leaving no reaction time.
2.45 Laboratory Discrepancies in Science Laboratory Register Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Laboratory stock registers should be properly maintained. During audit certain shortcomings were observed in this regard. Such as: Register was not validated by Competent Authority as per specified intervals. Breakage items were not recorded in the relevant register. Physical balances not matched with balances appearing in stock registers. Few of instances are as under; [Sr. No. | Item | Balance (Stock Reg.) | Physical Balance | Diff.
1 | Beakers 50ML | 44 | 41 | 03
2 | Beakers 500ML | 03 | 02 | 01
3 | Measuring Cylinder | 2 | 04 | 02]
2.46 Others Fuel Average Monitoring not Properly Conducted Med-High 🆕 New BH
Observation:
The OFA/AO is responsible for calculating fuel average of generator. Manager Admin Regional Office (RO) should also ensure timely communication of the generator’s standard fuel average to the Branch. During the audit it was observed that OFA was not properly monitoring the generator’s fuel consumption. The Regional Office had also not communicated the standard fuel average. As informed by the OFA: Fuel balance was mentioned on assumption. They were not checking the actual balance. Opening balance of fuel (on month start), and closing balance of fuel (on month end) was not accounted for during calculation of fuel consumption. This may lead to untracked variances in fuel efficiency.
2.47 Others Stock Register not Maintained for Used Engine Oil Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
During the audit, it was observed that the Stock Register for used Engine Oil was not maintained. No proper record was available regarding the quantity of used mobile oil received after oil change of generator, stored, or disposed.
2.48 Others Inward Gate Pass not in Practice Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
Gate Passes are the basic tool for controlling the inward /outward movement of items. However, during review it was observed that the practice for preparing inward gate pass was not implemented.
2.49 Others Incomplete List of Photocopier & Canteen Contractor Assets Low-Med 🆕 New BH
Observation:
During the audit, lists of photocopier, and the canteen contractor assets were found unsigned by the concerned parties, and the date on which assets were recorded/checked was also not mentioned.

This audit report covers 80 observations for Girls Campus Officers Colony, Multan (Audit visit: From 23 Feb to 06 Mar, 2026; Period covered: From 08 Sep, 2020 to 23, Feb 2026). Of these, 31 are Repeated (un-resolved from previous audit dated 17 December, 2020) and 49 are New observations identified during the current visit. Observations referred to Branch Head (BH): 68 | Regional Office (RO): 12.
Detailed Observations
🔁 Repeated Observations (Un-Resolved) — 31 items
1.1 Expenditure Exceeded the Budgeted Amount Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
More specific (micro level) audit findings, along-with recommendations thereon, are as following: Repeated Observations (Un-Resolved) Budgetary limits should be kept in view while making spending. Budgeting and analysis of variances therefrom, are useful monitoring and control tools that can help relevant authorities in the process of effective management and good governance. The review of system generated “Budget Variance Sheets” revealed that overspending was made under certain head of accounts. Few of such instances are shared as Annexure-A.
▶ Recommendation
As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/finance-audit/bank-cash-payment-process/- 3. Branch Operational Budget (Imprest): “Budget variance shall be monitored for judicious use of funds allocated under all Heads of Expenses. System generated budget variance sheet be consulted to monitor variance of expense against budget allocation and discrepancy, if any, rectified. “Branch Purchase Committee and certain analytical reports in the Module e.g. Imprest Budget Variance Statement shall provide support to BH in this regard.” As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/finance-audit/lfa-esl/ - 6. OPERATIONAL EXPENDITURE: “Monitoring of Opex (Imprest) variances of Branches shall be conducted at RO Finance on monthly basis.”
▶ Auditee Response
We will have a vigilant check to monitor the budget variance on monthly basis.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
No audit view recorded.
1.2 Budget for Student Expenditure Remained Unutilized Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
As per Company policy, savings under the category of student expenses should be avoided. During review of “Budget Variance Sheets”, it was observed that budget for student expenditure remained unutilized in certain heads. Few of such instances are shared as Annexure-B.
▶ Recommendation
As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/finance-audit/bank-cash-payment-process/ - 3. Branch Operational Budget (Imprest): “There should be no saving of funds under the category of ‘student expenses”.
▶ Auditee Response
We will have utilized student’s expenditure remaining budget in future.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
No audit view recorded.
1.3 Withholding Tax Deposited with Delay Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
As per Company policy, tax withheld from suppliers should be deposited within seven days. During review of ledger, delays were observed. Few of the instances are shared as Annexure-C.
▶ Recommendation
Tax policies should be properly followed, any lapses in this regard may result in penalties to the company. As per SMFO.HO letter dated 04 Jul, 2014: “Please follow the instructions at your end and also instruct the schools and other concerned staff to deduct the tax accordingly and deposit the same into Government Treasury within seven days from the deduction and payment”.
▶ Auditee Response
We apologize for late submission of withholding Tax. However, the policy has been implemented with immediate effect. We assure you that this observation will never be repeated again.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
No audit view recorded.
1.4 Reimbursements not Timely Processed by RO Low-Med 🔁 Repeated RO
Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
Branches are allocated a limited budget for each head of account, and reimbursement claims should be processed in a timely manner. However, based on the detail provided by the branch, delays were observed in the processing of claims. Few of the instances have been shared as Annexure-D.
▶ Recommendation
As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/finance-audit/reimbursement-process/#2--types-of-reimbursable-expenses - 4. Reimbursement Claim Procedure: “All reimbursement claims received shall be checked by respective Accounts department within 20 days of their receipt. SMF-RO and Manager Accounts at HO shall ensure reconciliation of inter office reimbursements balances on monthly basis”.
▶ Auditee Response
No auditee response recorded.
▶ RO Response
Please be informed that ROC Finance handles branch reimbursement cases in a systematic manner, prioritizing them among other tasks. As a result, 20-day completion timeline is not consistently achievable. Below are a few factors that cause deviations from the 20-day timeline. Some bills are based on purchase orders (POs) and are forwarded to the relevant departments (Procurement, Marketing, Construction) for PO generation, which takes additional processing time. Certain bills are received late from branches due to delays in completing the related activities and engagement in urgent tasks. Sometimes, priority items at desk is another factor of late processing the claims. Furthermore, in practical scenarios, the 20-day timeline is often insufficient for the majority of cases.
▶ Audit View
The number of days is mentioned in the policy. However, any changes required in this regard may be properly documented as feedback and coordinated for necessary updation (if applicable). As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/hrm/33-exceptions-amendments/ - 32. Exceptions & Amendments: “Any amendments and revisions to the Manual as deemed necessary shall be incorporated as per SOP no. S&A/SOP/003 for “Development and Amendment of Policies & Procedures”. (Extract from SOP no. S&A/SOP/003 ) As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/SOP-003-Final-pdf-5th-Nov-24.pdf: “4.2.2 Proposals for revisions/amendments of policies shall be discussed between Department Head / authorized representatives (Process Owner) and S&A team along with necessary explanations and justifications. 4.2.3 The Head S&A and Department Head shall evaluate the submitted proposal in the context of control requirements and objectives of the Organization.”
1.5 Balance Confirmation Certificates not Provided Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
For balance confirmation purposes, the Branch was requested for the balance confirmation certificates from the vendors with whom the business activities were done and staff to whom advances were issued (during the audit period). However, the balance confirmation certificates were awaited till audit completion.
▶ Recommendation
For clarity of payables/receivables recorded in books, the balance confirmation certificates should be provided.
▶ Auditee Response
We have received many balance certificates from vendors, we will ensure the remaining balance certificates till 30 April, 2026.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
As shared by the Branch, the certificates were in process of completion, till issuance of the report.
1.6 Accounting Procedure not Followed Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
All the supporting documents must be presented to the Branch Head along with the cheque, to ensure the compliance of procedures. However, it was observed that the Cheques were apparently prepared and issued without printing the related bank payment vouchers. Seemingly the vouchers were printed after making the transaction. The cheques were prepared and issued with printing bank payment vouchers and other supporting documents but due to closing purpose, the above-mentioned vouchers were made on back dates just because of busy schedule. We will be more vigilant in future.

1.7 Vendors Registration not Performed Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Branch Imprest, Payments & Accounts
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
As per policy, procurement shall be made from registered vendors. The registered vendor is a vendor for which Vendor Master Data is available in Vendor Register for Branches, based on approved VRF. However, the practice of vendor registration was not being properly followed, and the vendor register was not maintained.
▶ Recommendation
As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/procurement/sourcing-selection-registration-of-vendors/2/ - 2. Registration of Vendors: “The procurement shall be made from registered vendors. The registered vendor is a vendor for which Vendor Master Data is created ….. in Vendor Register for Branches based on approved VRF. The designated …. BPC shall complete the VRF, based on the criteria detailed above and sign the completed form. VRF and related documents, if any, shall be forwarded to Senior ….. BH for approval. …. BH shall review the documents submitted and provide approval on VRF for inclusion in Vendor ….. Register, upon satisfaction.”
▶ Auditee Response
Vendor registration is in process now. We will ensure strict compliance in future.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
No audit view recorded.
1.8 Employees Record of Social Security not Available Low-Med 🔁 Repeated BH
Recruitment, Payroll & Employee Record
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
As per Punjab Govt directions, employees having salary from Rs. 41,000 (or as prescribed by the notification issued time to time) should be registered with Punjab Employees Social Security Institution (providing hospitalization and dispensary facilities etc.) It was observed that PESSI record for the payments etc., was not available.
▶ Recommendation
Appropriate procedure should be devised and adopted at concerned levels regarding; Registration of employees. PESSI record. keeping (for instance PESSI Employee Cards). Monthly payment. Employees should also be informed about the benefits provided by PESSI for example medical care facilities and cash benefits to the secured workers and their dependents.
▶ Auditee Response
Registration of employees was recorded in the files as a copy of PESSI Card and was also presented on request. We did not have rights to access monthly payment record of emplyees. However Head Office was contacted for the guidance so that require data can be fetched from Beams and maintain at school level too.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
It is requested that the branch should be facilitated in this regard, so that the same may be referred, whenever required.

1.10 Outgoing Attendance not Marked by Staff Med-High 🔁 Repeated BH
Recruitment, Payroll & Employee Record
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
Attendance should be properly marked by all employees. As per BEAMS record, it was noted that outgoing attendance was not marked in certain cases. Due to the issue, compliance of office timings cannot be verified. The matter may affect also their Leave Encashment. Few of the instances has been shared in Annexure-F.
▶ Recommendation
Branch Management should monitor attendance record of the branch employees. Moreover, in case of out attendance not marked, staff should raise application for the attendance not marked, or early leaving (whichever is applicable) highlighting the reasons. As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/hrm/16-working-hours-attendance-2/2/#3--attendance - 3. Attendance: “Employees shall mark their attendance on biometric devices (online attendance) installed in offices.
▶ Auditee Response
Strict follow up will be ensured in future. In past we used be more vigilantly following up time in by referring to the attendance calendar of habitual late comers. However in future same practice will be adopted to monitor time out of all those who do not apply it on daily basis.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
No audit view recorded.
1.11 Delay in Final Settlement Cases Low-Med 🔁 Repeated RO
Recruitment, Payroll & Employee Record
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
Final settlement claims should be processed within prescribed timeline. As per the detail shared by Branch, final settlement claims of few employees were finalized with delays. Some of the instances are as under: [Sr.No. | Emp. ID | Employee Name | Resign Date | Last WorkDate | Final SettlementDate | DelayExc 21 days
1 | 88919 | Ms. Warda Rafique | 01.09.23 | 06.09.23 | 12.01.24 | 107
2 | 101983 | Ms. Sania Zubair | 13.06.25 | 08.05.25 | 25.08.25 | 88
3 | 105519 | Ms. Iqra Mohsin | 01.09.25 | 30.08.25 | 08.12.25 | 79
4 | 49279 | Ms. Azra Shaukat | 18.03.25 | 18.03.25 | 19.06.25 | 72
5 | 63012 | Mr. Faisal Mohayydin | 11.05.23 | 17.05.23 | 16.08.23 | 70
6 | 91930 | Ms. Hira Naveed | 20.08.24 | 16.08.24 | 14.11.24 | 69
7 | 79746 | Ms. Sidra Sami Ullah | 03.08.24 | 31.07.24 | 28.10.24 | 68
8 | 86040 | Ms. Mashaal Huma | 01.08.24 | 31.07.24 | 28.10.24 | 68
9 | 101472 | Ms. Narmeen Fatima | 22.08.24 | 21.08.24 | 14.11.24 | 64
10 | 106193 | Ms. Ushna Khan | 03.10.25 | 10.10.25 | 02.01.26 | 63]
▶ Recommendation
As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/hrm/14-clearance-final-settlement-2/ - 5. Timeline: “Keeping in view all the requirements, the clearance and final settlement process must not take more than three working weeks barring exceptions.”
▶ Auditee Response
We will ensure strict compliance for follow up of final settlement claims in future. ROC is also requested to finalize claims at their earliest.
▶ RO Response
The final settlement process through BEAMS has significantly enhanced efficiency, reducing turnaround times. However, delays in final settlements persist, particularly from July to October, primarily due to delays in profit calculations at the Corporate Finance end. Additionally, the online issuance of cheques—which takes over 2-3 weeks at the signatories end—further contributes to the delays. Instances of employees going on uninformed absences, long leaves, or not serving their notice period also result in delays, as resignation order/ end-of-employment issuances are processed based on their last working day. Furthermore, positions such as Librarian, and OFA that require proper handover processes tend to experience additional delays in the settlement process.
▶ Audit View
No audit view recorded.

▼ Finding
Employees should be confirmed after completion of probation. Scope: “All regular employees are required to complete an initial probationary period of six working months extendable for a period of three working months on grounds of performance issues supported by documented reasons/conditions.

Probation periods of these employees were extended and due to this their confirmation got late. ROC is also requested to issue confirmation letters without delay.
▶ RO Response
Teaching staff hired after January are confirmed after the summer break, once they have completed six months of service. The adhoc period is not counted when their status changes to regular.

1.13 Delay in Providing Livery Items Low 🔁 Repeated BH
Recruitment, Payroll & Employee Record
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
Livery items should be provided to junior staff on time, as directed in the policy. As per detail provided by the branch, delays were observed in providing the livery items. Few of the detail is as under: [Session | Uniform Was Due | Uniform Delivered | Delay (Month)
Winter 2025 | Sept.25 | 25.Dec | 3
Summer 2025 | Apr.25 | 25.Jul | 3
Winter 2024 | Sept.24 | 24.Nov | 2
Summer 2024 | Apr.24 | 24.May | 1
Winter 2023 | Sept.23 | 23.Dec | 3
Summer 2023 | Apr.23 | 23.May | 1
Winter 2022 | Sept.22 | 22.Nov | 2
Winter 2021 | Sept.21 | 21.Nov | 2
Winter 2020 | Sept.20 | Oct.20 | 1
Summer 2020 | Apr.20 | Jun.20 | 2]
▶ Recommendation
As per the company practice uniform is issued in April (for summer) and September (for winter). According to https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/hrm/7-staff-uniform-livery-policy/ - 2. Purchase of Livery: “The activity to procure livery shall be initiated considering the provision of summer uniform in the month of April and winter uniform in the month of September or in a specific month suitable to certain Offices/Branches as determined by the respective RD for their respective Region Office.” According to https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/hrm/7-staff-uniform-livery-policy/ - 1. Eligibility: “Livery (uniforms, shoes etc.) shall be provided to regular/contractual “entitled employees” in accordance with their entitlement.
▶ Auditee Response
The observation is noted. The delay occurred due to oversight at OFA level. The branch OFA acknowledges his responsibility for providing livery items to support staff as per policy. Important protocols to be shared with vendor to ensure smooth transition between order date and delivery date. In addition to it, a vigilant follow up will also be kept to ensure compliance with the policy and avoid such delays in the future.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
No audit view recorded.

🆕 New Observations — 49 items
2.1 Significant Anomalies in Processing of Paint Bills Med-High 🆕 New BH
Branch Imprest, Payments& Accounts
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
During the audit, it was observed that paint bills were processed without proper verification of measurements claimed by paint contractor and related deductions. Few of such discrepancies included: Glass affixed in doors was not deducted from the painted area. White boards installed on the walls were also not properly deducted from the painted area. For example, in the branch some of the whiteboards were affixed partially on surfaces painted with emulsion and partially on surfaces painted with enamel. However, the deductions were not made in accordance with the applicable paint type (i.e. emulsion or enamel)/rate. As informed by Branch Personnel: Verification of the paint material by minimum two members of the Purchase Committee (PC) was not conducted to ensure that the supplied material complied with the approved specifications. The paint brand and quality were also not verified by AMCO, prior to application. The paint bills were processed without verification by AMCO. Signed delivery challans were not available to verify documentation regarding the receipt of materials. No documentary evidence was available to verify that approval had been obtained for the application of the second coat after completion of the first, as required under the prescribed process. Verification of the application of double coats was not carried out by the Admin and Accounts. Weather Shield was applied in certain areas before the prescribed period (of three years); however, no exception approval from the competent authority was obtained for the early reapplication. Paint Brand was not mentioned on the bills, and quotations.
▶ Recommendation
Paint bills should be properly checked prior to processing. All measurements should be verified before processing of paint bills. Any excess amount identified should be recovered from the vendor. Deductions for glass panels, white boards, and other non-painted areas should be properly done. Paint brand, quality, and material receipt should be verified before and during application. Necessary approvals and supporting documents should be maintained to verify the compliance of prescribed policies and procedures.
▶ Auditee Response
Strict compliance in future.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
During re-verification of paint bills by the branch an amount of appr. Rs. 30,000 was identified as paid in excess. As per email from Mr. Hussain Farooq (hussainfarooq.55130@bh.edu.pk), dated Apr 30, 2026: “PFA scanned copies of Annual Paint Bill measurements difference after Audit team guidance, we rechecked the actual measurements of annual paint bill and verified by our AMCO. The difference amount of Year 2023, 2024 and 2025 recoverable from Paint Vendor as under:

2.8 Incongruities in Preparation & Filing of Purchase Orders Low 🆕 New BH
Branch Imprest, Payments& Accounts
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
Purchase orders are basic document for purchases and acquisition of services. As per the standard practice, the PO/WO should be numbered, and a copy should be filed in sequential order. In addition to this, the company policy prescribes to obtain vendor acceptance, for written agreement of vendor(s) to the prescribed terms and conditions. However, it was identified that such practices were not being properly followed.
▶ Recommendation
As per standard practice, the purchase orders/work orders should be prenumbered, and sequentially filed once acknowledged by vendors, and issued to them. As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/procurement/procurement-of-goods-services/3/#10--issuance-of-po--wo - 10. Issuance of PO / WO: “PO shall be handed over / forwarded to vendor, by the designated Official of SC / BA for vendor’s acceptance.” “All documents pertaining to the procurement shall be kept by …….. In case of Branches the said record shall be maintained by the respective BA.” “The format of Manual PO is attached as Purchase Order (Manual).”
▶ Auditee Response
Strict follow up will be ensured in future. Cash and Bank
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.

School shall send a circular to parents containing details of the activity (event, occurrence date, amount, last date of payment) to obtain their consent and identify potential participants. Collection of SF shall be made by the concerned Class Teacher (CT) in accordance with the timelines mentioned in the circular. Class Teacher shall obtain a “Class List” (CL) from the System and record the amount received against the name of each student. The CT shall total the amounts received, mention the activity and sign the list. The CT shall then submit the amount collected along with the CL to Coordinator within two days from the last date mentioned in the Circular. The Coordinator shall prepare and sign the summary of amount collected and hand over the summary, amount and CLs to Branch Accountant (BA) against his/her acknowledgement on copy of the summary sheet. Branch Accountant shall keep memorandum record of the cash collected and the amount expended for the approved activity. Statement of cash collected and the amount expended shall be duly signed by the school/ section head. Activities should be planned in a way that cash collection exactly matches the amount of relevant expenditure. In case some amount is left in the balance, the same should be deposited in bank account promptly by the BA, duly reconciled with the memorandum record. The school will bear the cost of transportation for field trips, whereas, if needed, the students can share partial costs.” As per As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/finance-audit-cash-collection-at-branches Submission of Expenses Adjustment: “Branch Accountant shall prepare an Excel Summary of receipts & expenses for each activity and submit it to BH on a monthly basis. All documents, vouchers and bills of expenses incurred from SF shall be submitted to RO Finance on quarterly basis. RO shall check the expenses and file the “SF Imprest” separately from other ESL Imprest Statements. Any observations shall be resolved in coordination with the concerned Cluster Convener.” As per As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/finance-audit-cash-collection-at-branches/ Record Keeping: “Record shall be kept in safe custody in accordance with the record retention policy of the Company.” Due to unavailability of record and the highlighted issues, the cash collection could not be verified. There is need to complete the record on urgent basis, and submit in RO for checking.
▶ Auditee Response
We will follow strictly the policies and complete the record as soon as possible.

2.10 Significant Anomalies in Bake Sales Record High 🆕 New BH
Cash & Bank
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
During the audit, significant non-compliance with prescribed policies and deficiencies in documentation related to Bake Sale activities were identified. These included: Detail of Bake Sales was not properly maintained. Bake Sale Activity for 2022 was still open in “Cash Collection Module”, and there was no record available. For the year 2025 & 2026: Committee formation details for Bake Sale activities were not available. Serial Number Receipt Book record was not available. Detailed cash collection record was not available for verification, including (but not limited to): Collections, donations, and the balances etc. Compliance with price ceiling of PKR 200 per item. Compliance with student cash limit of PKR 500 per student. Activities were not recorded in “Cash Collection Module”. Approval from BH (after closure of activity) was not available. Receipt for Rs. 50,000 donated to SKMT (Year 2025) was not available. Balances from Bake Sale e.g. Rs. 2,420 (Feb 2025), and Rs. 20,710 (Jan 2026) were not recorded in module.
▶ Recommendation
Company policies should be followed and the record should be properly maintained. As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/events/bake-sale/4/ - Administration of Cash Collection: “The school must devise ways to administer the cash collected during the bake sale. The school head is responsible to make committees for the collection of cash which should be properly documented. The branch accountant should not be part of the said committee. The committee should stay mobile throughout the event for cash management. The committee should use a serially numbered receipt book for the purpose of cash collection from the students. The collected cash should be handed over to the branch accountant who would ensure its proper recording in the cash collection module as well as maintenance of manual memorandum record as per the cash collection policy. In-house set-up is suggested to hold the bake sale. In case any expense is carried out for the set-up purpose e.g. tentage, tables etc., such expenses, however, will not be loaded on the Branch imprest. The expense will be adjusted from the cash collected from the students. In summation, the cash to be handed over to the charity will be net-off such expenses. The branch accountant should submit the activity account at the closure of the activity to the School Head for her/ his approval and closure of the activity thereafter. The school management must take students on board as to who the recipients of this charity are. The school may conduct an assembly and invite the recipients and/or send the student representatives over with the charity. Bake Sale Guidelines for Beaconhouse.” As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/events/bake-sale/2/ - 5. Rules and Procedures: “Communication: Schools must set up a cash limit for the students attending the bake sale as an audience. Cash per student may be limited to PKR 500 for the bake sale donation drive. Price Ceiling: School must establish a price limit for the items on sale. Cost per item should not exceed PKR 200. In order to serve umpteem in a restricted cost bracket the item size can be aligned.” Due to unavailability of record and the highlighted issues, the cash collection from Bake Sales could not be verified. There is need to complete the record on urgent basis, and submit in RO for checking.
▶ Auditee Response
We will strictly follow the polices and balance amount of both years Bake Sale will be deposited in Taare Zameen par trust.
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
No audit view recorded.

Automation of Student Transfer File: Upon final approval from the RO, in the system, a ticket shall be auto generated against the student ID. The ticket shall need to be filled by the PRO with the relevant consignment number and courier company through which the transfer file is being dispatched. Upon receiving the transfer file, the acquiring school will need to close this ticket. The turnaround time for this is 1 week. Post TAT alert will be sent to the PRO and BH to expedite the dispatch of such files. Student Files: “In case of transfer of a student, it shall be the responsibility of the PRO to promptly send the student file to the concerned Branch after entering information in student module.”

2.46 Fuel Average Monitoring not Properly Conducted Med-High 🆕 New BH
Others
↑ Back to Synopsis
▼ Finding
The OFA/AO is responsible for calculating fuel average of generator. Manager Admin Regional Office (RO) should also ensure timely communication of the generator’s standard fuel average to the Branch. During the audit it was observed that OFA was not properly monitoring the generator’s fuel consumption. The Regional Office had also not communicated the standard fuel average. As informed by the OFA: Fuel balance was mentioned on assumption. They were not checking the actual balance. Opening balance of fuel (on month start), and closing balance of fuel (on month end) was not accounted for during calculation of fuel consumption. This may lead to untracked variances in fuel efficiency.
▶ Recommendation
As per https://index.beaconhouse.net/kb/manuals/construction-renovation-maintenance/generator-repair-hiring/ - 2. Record Keeping (Logbook): “OFA/AO shall be informed about the standard fuel average of the genset by the relevant Manager Admin RO. OFA/AO shall calculate the fuel average of the generator on the basis of entries recorded in the logbook, after regular intervals (preferable at the time of new fueling). Fuel Average = Fuel Consumption ÷ Running Hours”
▶ Auditee Response
The observation is noted. The OFA was maintaining records as per previous practice. However, the branch does not have the equipment to verify the generator’s exit fuel average. As per the audit recommendation, the branch will ensure that records are maintained in accordance with the policy and coordinate with the RO to receive the standard fuel average for proper monitoring. Going forward, the branch will implement all feasible measures to track fuel consumption accurately within the available resources.”
▶ RO Response
No ro response recorded.
▶ Audit View
No audit view recorded.
